**Summary.** This paper reports the discovery of stable even-denominator Fractional Quantum Hall States at $\nu=3/4$ and $5/4$ in GaAs quantum wells. The findings are explained by a theoretical model involving two-component bilayer composite fermions, highlighting that these states rely critically on maintaining symmetry in the electron charge distribution.

**Why it may be interesting.** This work provides detailed experimental evidence for exotic correlated electron phases (FQHSs) that emerge due to the interplay between orbital quantization (Landau levels), strong Coulomb interactions, and structural degrees of freedom (bilayer coupling).

<details><summary>Detailed structure</summary>

**Main problem.** The research focuses on discovering and characterizing novel even-denominator Fractional Quantum Hall States (FQHSs) at filling factors $\nu=3/4$ and $5/4$. These states are studied in the context of wide quantum wells exhibiting bilayer characteristics.

**Main result.** Robust even-denominator FQHSs are observed, which are explained by a two-component, bilayer composite fermion framework. The stability of these states is highly dependent on maintaining charge distribution symmetry.

**Method.** The study combines low-temperature transport measurements ($R_{xx}, R_{xy}$) with theoretical modeling using composite fermion theory and analyzing the system's response to controlled charge asymmetry.

**Model / system.** The physical platform is a two-dimensional electron system (2DES) confined in wide GaAs quantum wells, which naturally supports a bilayer charge distribution. The physics is governed by strong Coulomb interactions and tunable interlayer tunneling.

**Key observables.** Longitudinal resistance minima ($R_{xx}$), energy gaps ($\nu\Delta$), and the critical charge asymmetry $(\Delta n/n)_C$ required to destabilize the FQHSs.

**Important parameters / regimes.** Filling factors $\nu=3/4, 5/4, 3/2$; interlayer tunneling strength ($\alpha$); charge distribution symmetry ($\Delta n/n$).

**Assumptions / limitations.** The analysis assumes that the FQHSs can be described by generalizations of composite fermion wavefunctions (e.g., Scarola-Jain bilayer framework).

**Figures summary.** Figures illustrate $R_{xx}$ and $R_{xy}$ traces confirming FQHS plateaus at $\nu=3/2, 5/4,$ and $3/4$, and show how these states evolve or disappear when the charge distribution symmetry is intentionally broken.

**Paper structure.** The paper progresses by first establishing the experimental observation of even-denominator FQHSs in wide QWs. It then develops a theoretical explanation linking these states to bilayer composite fermion physics, detailing their dependence on interlayer tunneling and charge symmetry.

</details>

<details><summary>Abstract</summary>

Two-dimensional electron systems (2DESs) confined to wide GaAs quantum wells provide a unique platform to study exotic fractional quantum Hall states (FQHSs) because the 2DES has a bilayer charge distribution with significant interlayer tunneling. Precise control over the 2DES density allows the tuning of the interlayer tunneling over a wide range. Here, we present our discovery of new even-denominator FQHSs in the lowest Landau level (orbital index \textit{N} = 0) at filling factors $ν= 3/4$ and 5/4 in an ultrahigh-quality 2DES confined to a 72.5-nm-wide GaAs quantum well. The ground states at $ν= 3/4$ and 5/4 both evolve from composite fermion Fermi seas to FQHSs as the density is raised so that interlayer tunneling is sufficiently reduced and the 2DES becomes two-component, signaled by the behavior of the FQHSs flanking $ν= 3/4$ and 5/4. The two-component nature of the $ν=3/4$ and 5/4 FQHSs is also evident from their extreme sensitivity to the bilayer charge distribution symmetry: both states disappear quickly when the charge distribution is made asymmetric by only $\simeq 2\%$. We find a natural explanation for the 3/4 and 5/4 FQHSs in terms of two states linked by particle-hole symmetry, and using the Scarola-Jain bilayer composite fermion framework which is a generalization of the well-known, two-component, Halperin state ($Ψ_{331}$ state). Our observations elucidate the crucial role of competing energy and length scales in wide quantum wells in stabilizing new ground states.

</details>

**Summary.** The research investigates how pressure influences the superfluid transition temperature splitting of $^3$He confined in nematic aerogels. By comparing results from samples coated with $	ext{}^4	ext{He}$ versus pure $^3$He, the authors test theoretical models describing boundary effects. The findings suggest that coating the pores leads to a more theoretically consistent description of the phase transitions under pressure.

**Why it may be interesting.** This work bridges condensed matter physics (superfluidity, phase transitions) with materials science (porous media confinement), providing experimental validation for complex boundary condition effects on quantum fluid behavior.

<details><summary>Detailed structure</summary>

**Main problem.** The study investigates how external pressure affects the splitting of the superfluid transition temperature ($T_c$) in $^3$He confined within anisotropic nematic aerogels.

**Main result.** The presence of a $	ext{}^4	ext{He}$ coating on the aerogel strands leads to a more symmetric field dependence for the transition splitting, which shows better agreement with theoretical predictions at higher applied pressures compared to pure $^3$He samples.

**Method.** Measurements were performed using a vibrating wire (VW) resonator and quartz tuning fork for thermometry while applying varying magnetic fields and pressures. Transition temperatures were determined by analyzing resonance frequency and Full Width at Half-Maximum (FWHM).

**Model / system.** The system is superfluid $^3$He confined in nematic aerogels, comparing boundary conditions: specular reflection (with $	ext{}^4	ext{He}$ coating) versus diffuse scattering/impurity effects (pure $^3$He). Theoretical models include Ginzburg-Landau coefficients and impurity magnetization descriptions.

**Key observables.** Superfluid transition temperature splitting ($\Delta T$), resonance frequency, Full Width at Half Maximum (FWHM), and the ratio of slopes of the transitions with respect to magnetic field ($H$).

**Important parameters / regimes.** Applied pressure (e.g., 7.1 bar, 15.4 bar, 19.4 bar) and applied magnetic field ($H$ up to $31 	ext{ kOe}$).

**Assumptions / limitations.** The analysis distinguishes between boundary scattering mechanisms ($	ext{}^4	ext{He}$ coating vs. pure $	ext{}^3	ext{He}$) and compares observed nonlinearities against established theoretical models.

**Figures summary.** Figures illustrate the temperature dependence of FWHM and resonance frequency for both coated and uncoated samples at various pressures, highlighting the distinct transition regimes ($T_{ca1}, T_{ca2}$ vs. $T_{P1}, T_{P2}$).

**Paper structure.** The paper systematically compares experimental results under varying pressure and boundary conditions ($	ext{}^4	ext{He}$ coating vs. pure $	ext{}^3	ext{He}$) to resolve discrepancies between previous measurements and theoretical predictions regarding $T_c$ splitting.

</details>

<details><summary>Abstract</summary>

Here, we present the results of an investigation of how the pressure affects the splitting of the superfluid transition temperature in $^3$He in anisotropic aerogel. It is well known that boundary conditions significantly influence the properties of superfluid phases in aerogel. When aerogel strands are coated with a $^4$He layer in magnetic field, new phases, such as the polar, polar-distorted A (DA), polar-distorted B (DB), and $β$ phases, become energetically favorable. In contrast, without this coating, the system tends to favor phases resembling the bulk A, B, and A$_1$ phases. Our earlier results showed a nonlinear dependence of the superfluid transition temperature splitting in pure $^3$He, but the range of nonlinearity did not match the theoretical predictions based on magnetic scattering effects. To further investigate this discrepancy, we performed measurements of the splitting under varying pressures for both pure $^3$He and with $^4$He coverage of the aerogel strands.

</details>
